## Summary of Exploration

I've thoroughly explored the ccs-viewer Rust project. Here are the answers to your questions:

### 1. JSON Parsing Library Used

**Library: `serde_json` (version 1.x)**

This is declared in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/Cargo.toml`:
```toml
serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
serde_json = "1"
```

The project uses `serde` for serialization/deserialization with the derive feature enabled, and `serde_json` for JSON-specific handling.

### 2. JSON Parsing Usage (Main Parsing Paths)

There are **two primary JSON parsing paths**:

**Path 1: JSONL Records (standard Claude Code session files)**
- Located in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:331`
- Parse method: `serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line)` — parses each line into a `Record` enum
- The `Record` enum is defined in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/types.rs` using internally-tagged deserialization (`#[serde(tag = "type")]`)
- Handles 10 record types: file-history-snapshot, user, assistant, progress, last-prompt, queue-operation, system, custom-title, agent-name, summary
- Includes error handling with detailed error collection

**Path 2: Agent Meta Files (standalone JSON, not JSONL)**
- Located in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:249`
- Parse method: `serde_json::from_reader(file)` — deserializes into `AgentMeta` struct
- These are single JSON objects (not JSONL), identified by `.meta.json` filename suffix
- Contains agent type and optional description

**Sniff Test (filtering non-Claude-Code JSONL)**
- Located in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:310-311`
- Quick check before full parsing: only process JSONL lines starting with `{"type":` or `{"parentUuid":`
- This prevents parsing unrelated JSONL files (logs, benchmarks, etc.)

**Parse Error Handling**
- Located in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:335-345`
- When a line fails to parse as a full `Record`, it attempts to parse as raw JSON to extract the `type` field for error reporting
- Errors are deduplicated and grouped by message + record type

### 3. What the `-r` Flag Does

**Flag: `--recursive` / `-r`**
- Location: `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:36-38`
- **Effect**: Changes how positional arguments are interpreted

**Without `-r`** (default):
- Positional args are treated as file glob patterns
- Example: `ccs-viewer "data/*.jsonl"` expands the glob directly

**With `-r`** (recursive mode):
- Positional args are treated as directory paths (or directory globs)
- Recursively searches directories using `**/<pattern>` expansion
- Default file patterns: `*.jsonl` and `agent-*.meta.json`
- Can be overridden with `--glob <PAT>` (repeatable)
- Example: `ccs-viewer -r .claude` finds all `.jsonl` and `.meta.json` files under `.claude/` recursively

**Implementation** (lines 73-114 in main.rs):
- Validates directories exist and are actually directories
- For each directory, expands file globs recursively using the `glob` crate
- Deduplicates and sorts results

### 4. Performance-Related Code and Comments

**Key Performance Observations:**

1. **Streaming JSONL Processing**
   - Location: `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:296-363`
   - Uses `BufReader` for line-by-line processing instead of loading entire files into memory
   - Processes each line independently with `serde_json::from_str()` — naturally streaming

2. **Sniff Test Optimization**
   - Location: `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:299-317`
   - Performs a cheap first-line check before processing the entire file
   - Only parses as full JSON if first line matches Claude Code session pattern
   - Skips non-matching files completely (early exit)

3. **Error Aggregation Strategy**
   - Location: `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:225-225`
   - Uses `HashMap` for deduplicating and grouping errors rather than storing individual errors
   - Sorts by count at display time (lines 395-396) rather than during processing

4. **File Deduplication**
   - Location: `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs:181-182`
   - Sorts and deduplicates file list after globbing: `files.sort(); files.dedup();`

5. **No Comments about Performance Issues**
   - The codebase contains no TODO comments or FIXME notes about performance optimization
   - The approach is fundamentally sound: stream-based processing with early filtering

**File Paths:**
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/Cargo.toml` — Dependency declarations
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs` — Entry point with CLI, file resolution, and parsing logic
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/types.rs` — Type definitions and deserialization configuration
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/README.md` — CLI usage documentation

# json-steroids: High-Performance JSON for Rust

## Overview

json-steroids is a fast JSON serialization and deserialization library for Rust that emphasizes zero-copy parsing and efficient memory management. It provides derive macros for automatic implementation of serialization and deserialization traits.

## Key Features

The library includes several standout capabilities:

- **Zero-copy string parsing**: "Strings without escape sequences are borrowed directly from input, avoiding unnecessary allocations"
- **Fast serialization** with pre-allocated buffers and optimized number formatting
- **Derive macros** for automatic trait implementation
- **Minimal dependencies** (only itoa and ryu for number formatting)
- **Full JSON support** including Unicode escapes and surrogate pairs
- **Pretty printing** with optional indentation
- **Dynamic parsing** via the flexible JsonValue type

## Installation

Add to your `Cargo.toml`:

```toml
[dependencies]
json-steroids = "0.1.2"
```

## Usage Example

```rust
use json_steroids::{Json, to_string, from_str};

#[derive(Json)]
struct Person {
    name: String,
    age: u32,
    email: Option<String>,
}

fn main() {
    let person = Person {
        name: "Alice".to_string(),
        age: 30,
        email: Some("alice@example.com".to_string()),
    };
    let json = to_string(&person);
    println!("{}", json);
    
    let parsed: Person = from_str(json_str).unwrap();
}
```

## API Functions

**Serialization:**
- `to_string<T>()` - compact JSON output
- `to_string_pretty<T>()` - indented output

**Deserialization:**
- `from_str<T>()` - parse from string slice
- `from_bytes<T>()` - parse from bytes
- `parse()` - dynamic parsing into JsonValue

## Performance Benchmarks

json-steroids significantly outperforms serde_json in most scenarios:

- **Zero-copy operations**: "1.7-2.7x faster for zero-copy operations with Cow<'de, str>" and borrowed strings
- **Serialization**: 17-88% faster for typical workloads
- **Deserialization**: 20-70% faster, particularly strong on complex structures
- **Large arrays**: 67% faster deserialization
- **Dynamic parsing**: 60% faster when structure is unknown

The library wins on 14 of 17 benchmarks (82%), with serde_json only winning on plain string serialization tasks.
```

     1→//! Serde types for Claude Code session JSONL files.
     2→//!
     3→//! Each line of a session file is a JSON object with a `"type"` field that
     4→//! determines its shape. The [`Record`] enum uses serde's internally tagged
     5→//! representation (`#[serde(tag = "type")]`) to dispatch to the correct
     6→//! variant struct.
     7→//!
     8→//! # Key patterns
     9→//!
    10→//! **Label strings** — The `RECORD_LABELS` const is the single source of
    11→//! truth for type name strings (e.g. `"assistant"`, `"system"`).
    12→//! [`Record::label()`] indexes into it; [`Record::all_labels()`] returns
    13→//! the full slice. This avoids duplicating strings between the two methods.
    14→//!
    15→//! **Struct field ordering** — Each struct lists required fields first,
    16→//! then `Option` fields below a separator comment. This makes it visually
    17→//! obvious which fields need entries in the `optional_fields()` list.
    18→//!
    19→//! **`deny_unknown_fields`** — Every struct has `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`
    20→//! so that any JSON key not mapped to a Rust field causes a deserialization
    21→//! error. This ensures we know immediately when Claude Code adds new fields.
    22→//!
    23→//! **`optional_fields()`** — Structs with `Option` fields have a companion
    24→//! `optional_fields()` method listing the camelCase (or snake_case) JSON
    25→//! names of those fields. The `all_optional_fields_seen` test uses these
    26→//! lists to verify every `Option` field is `Some` at least once in test
    27→//! data. Nested fields use dot notation (`"message.usage.speed"`) and
    28→//! array filtering uses brackets (`"message.content[tool_use].caller"`).
    29→//!
    30→//! **Warning**: The `optional_fields()` lists are static — adding an
    31→//! `Option` field to a struct without updating the list means that field
    32→//! silently goes untested. The separator comment in each struct serves as
    33→//! a reminder.
    34→
    35→use std::collections::HashMap;
    36→
    37→use serde::Deserialize;
    38→use serde_json::Value;
    39→
    40→/// Wrapper for JSON data we haven't given a typed struct yet.
    41→/// Signals "structured but not yet typed" — distinguishes from
    42→/// deliberately modeled fields. Grep for `Untyped` to find all
    43→/// remaining untyped fields.
    44→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    45→pub struct Untyped(#[allow(dead_code)] Value);
    46→
    47→/// Top-level record: each line of a Claude Code session JSONL file
    48→/// deserializes into one of these variants, discriminated by the `type` field.
    49→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
    50→#[serde(tag = "type")]
    51→pub enum Record {
    52→    #[serde(rename = "file-history-snapshot")]
    53→    FileHistorySnapshot(FileHistorySnapshotRecord),
    54→
    55→    #[serde(rename = "user")]
    56→    User(Box<UserRecord>),
    57→
    58→    #[serde(rename = "assistant")]
    59→    Assistant(Box<AssistantRecord>),
    60→
    61→    #[serde(rename = "progress")]
    62→    Progress(Box<ProgressRecord>),
    63→
    64→    #[serde(rename = "last-prompt")]
    65→    LastPrompt(LastPromptRecord),
    66→
    67→    #[serde(rename = "queue-operation")]
    68→    QueueOperation(QueueOperationRecord),
    69→
    70→    #[serde(rename = "system")]
    71→    System(Box<SystemRecord>),
    72→
    73→    #[serde(rename = "custom-title")]
    74→    CustomTitle(CustomTitleRecord),
    75→
    76→    #[serde(rename = "agent-name")]
    77→    AgentName(AgentNameRecord),
    78→
    79→    #[serde(rename = "summary")]
    80→    Summary(SummaryRecord),
    81→}
    82→
    83→/// Single source of truth for record type label strings.
    84→const RECORD_LABELS: &[&str] = &[
    85→    "file-history-snapshot",
    86→    "user",
    87→    "assistant",
    88→    "progress",
    89→    "last-prompt",
    90→    "queue-operation",
    91→    "system",
    92→    "custom-title",
    93→    "agent-name",
    94→    "summary",
    95→];
    96→
    97→impl Record {
    98→    /// Returns the type label string for this record variant.
    99→    pub fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
   100→        RECORD_LABELS[match self {
   101→            Record::FileHistorySnapshot(_) => 0,
   102→            Record::User(_) => 1,
   103→            Record::Assistant(_) => 2,
   104→            Record::Progress(_) => 3,
   105→            Record::LastPrompt(_) => 4,
   106→            Record::QueueOperation(_) => 5,
   107→            Record::System(_) => 6,
   108→            Record::CustomTitle(_) => 7,
   109→            Record::AgentName(_) => 8,
   110→            Record::Summary(_) => 9,
   111→        }]
   112→    }
   113→
   114→    /// Returns the full list of known record type labels.
   115→    pub fn all_labels() -> &'static [&'static str] {
   116→        RECORD_LABELS
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    /// Returns the optional fields list for each record type that has them.
   120→    /// Used by tests to verify all Option fields are exercised.
   121→    pub fn optional_fields_by_type() -> Vec<(&'static str, &'static [&'static str])> {
   122→        vec![
   123→            ("user", UserRecord::optional_fields()),
   124→            ("assistant", AssistantRecord::optional_fields()),
   125→            ("progress", ProgressRecord::optional_fields()),
   126→            ("queue-operation", QueueOperationRecord::optional_fields()),
   127→            ("system", SystemRecord::optional_fields()),
   128→        ]
   129→    }
   130→}

   132→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   134→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   135→
   136→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   137→#[serde(deny_unknown_fields, rename_all = "camelCase")]

   143→

   151→

   195→// WARNING: When adding an Option field above, add its camelCase JSON name here.
   196→// This list cannot detect missing entries — unlisted fields silently go untested.
   197→impl UserRecord {

   232→/// User message content is either a plain text string (initial prompt)
   233→/// or an array of content blocks (tool results, text, etc.).
   234→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   235→#[serde(untagged)]

   240→
   241→/// A block within a user message content array.
   242→#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
   243→#[serde(tag = "type")]
   244→pub enum UserContentBlock {
   245→    #[serde(rename = "tool_result")]
   246→    ToolResult {

   663→#[cfg(test)]
   664→mod tests {
   665→    use super::*;
   666→    use std::collections::HashSet;
   667→    use std::fs::File;
   668→    use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};
   669→
   670→    const TEST_FILES: &[&str] = &[
   671→        "data/31ba272b-f7c2-436b-a017-269e27a64d07.jsonl",
   672→        "data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl",
   673→        "data/092de687-cd0d-4583-b872-bc2908dff3ba.jsonl",
   674→        "data/86fb7a89-abfa-4e84-b862-5983e93c0b3b.jsonl",
   675→        "data/ccs-viewer-tests.jsonl",
   676→        "data/agent-test.jsonl",
   677→    ];
   678→
   679→    fn deserialize_file(path: &str) -> usize {
   680→        deserialize_file_collecting(path, None)
   681→    }
   682→
   683→    fn deserialize_file_collecting(path: &str, mut labels: Option<&mut HashSet<String>>) -> usize {
   684→        let file = File::open(path).expect("sample JSONL file should exist");
   685→        let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   686→        let mut count = 0;
   687→        for (i, line) in reader.lines().enumerate() {
   688→            let line = line.unwrap();
   689→            if line.trim().is_empty() {
   690→                continue;
   691→            }
   692→            let result = serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line);
   693→            assert!(
   694→                result.is_ok(),
   695→                "line {} failed: {}",
   696→                i + 1,
   697→                result.unwrap_err()
   698→            );
   699→            if let (Ok(record), Some(labels)) = (&result, labels.as_deref_mut()) {
   700→                labels.insert(record.label().to_string());
   701→            }
   702→            count += 1;
   703→        }
   704→        count
   705→    }
   706→
   707→    #[test]
   708→    fn deserialize_sample_31ba() {
   709→        assert_eq!(
   710→            deserialize_file("data/31ba272b-f7c2-436b-a017-269e27a64d07.jsonl"),
   711→            22
   712→        );
   713→    }
   714→
   715→    #[test]
   716→    fn deserialize_sample_997a() {
   717→        assert_eq!(
   718→            deserialize_file("data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl"),
   719→            461
   720→        );
   721→    }
   722→
   723→    #[test]
   724→    fn deserialize_sample_092d() {
   725→        assert_eq!(
   726→            deserialize_file("data/092de687-cd0d-4583-b872-bc2908dff3ba.jsonl"),
   727→            224
   728→        );
   729→    }
   730→
   731→    #[test]
   732→    fn deserialize_sample_86fb() {
   733→        assert_eq!(
   734→            deserialize_file("data/86fb7a89-abfa-4e84-b862-5983e93c0b3b.jsonl"),
   735→            301
   736→        );
   737→    }
   738→
   739→    /// Look up a dotted path in a serde_json::Value.
   740→    /// Supports segments like "message", "usage", or array filtering
   741→    /// like "content[tool_use]" (find element in array where type == "tool_use").
   742→    fn value_at_path<'a>(val: &'a Value, path: &str) -> Option<&'a Value> {
   743→        let mut current = val;
   744→        for segment in path.split('.') {
   745→            if let Some(idx) = segment.find('[') {
   746→                let key = &segment[..idx];
   747→                let filter_type = &segment[idx + 1..segment.len() - 1];
   748→                let arr = current.get(key)?.as_array()?;
   749→                current = arr
   750→                    .iter()
   751→                    .find(|el| el.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) == Some(filter_type))?;
   752→            } else {
   753→                current = current.get(segment)?;
   754→            }
   755→        }
   756→        if current.is_null() {
   757→            None
   758→        } else {
   759→            Some(current)
   760→        }
   761→    }
   762→
   763→    /// Verify every Option field in every struct has at least one non-null
   764→    /// occurrence across test data. This catches Option fields that are defined
   765→    /// in a struct but never actually exercised by any test file.
   766→    ///
   767→    /// WARNING: This test uses a static list of Option fields per record type
   768→    /// (see each struct's `optional_fields()` method). It cannot catch Option
   769→    /// fields that are missing from those lists — those fields will silently
   770→    /// remain untested (always None). When adding a new Option field to a
   771→    /// struct, you must also add its camelCase JSON name to the corresponding
   772→    /// `optional_fields()` list.
   773→    #[test]
   774→    fn all_optional_fields_seen() {
   775→        let field_map = Record::optional_fields_by_type();
   776→        let mut seen: HashSet<(String, String)> = HashSet::new();
   777→
   778→        for path in TEST_FILES {
   779→            let file = File::open(path).expect("test file should exist");
   780→            let reader = BufReader::new(file);
   781→            for line in reader.lines() {
   782→                let line = line.unwrap();
   783→                if line.trim().is_empty() {
   784→                    continue;
   785→                }
   786→                let val: Value = match serde_json::from_str(&line) {
   787→                    Ok(v) => v,
   788→                    Err(_) => continue,
   789→                };
   790→                let record_type = match val.get("type").and_then(|t| t.as_str()) {
   791→                    Some(t) => t,
   792→                    None => continue,
   793→                };
   794→                for &(rtype, fields) in &field_map {
   795→                    if rtype != record_type {
   796→                        continue;
   797→                    }
   798→                    for field in fields {
   799→                        if value_at_path(&val, field).is_some() {
   800→                            seen.insert((record_type.to_string(), field.to_string()));
   801→                        }
   802→                    }
   803→                }
   804→            }
   805→        }
   806→
   807→        let mut never_seen = Vec::new();
   808→        for &(rtype, fields) in &field_map {
   809→            for field in fields {
   810→                if !seen.contains(&(rtype.to_string(), field.to_string())) {
   811→                    never_seen.push(format!("{rtype}.{field}"));
   812→                }
   813→            }
   814→        }
   815→        never_seen.sort();
   816→        assert!(
   817→            never_seen.is_empty(),
   818→            "Option fields never seen as Some in test data: {never_seen:?}"
   819→        );
   820→    }
   821→
   822→    #[test]
   823→    fn deserialize_agent_jsonl() {
   824→        assert_eq!(deserialize_file("data/agent-test.jsonl"), 7);
   825→    }
   826→
   827→    #[test]
   828→    fn deserialize_agent_meta() {
   829→        let path = "data/agent-adf742daa2c66fe48.meta.json";
   830→        let file = File::open(path).expect("agent meta file should exist");
   831→        let meta: AgentMeta = serde_json::from_reader(file).expect("agent meta should deserialize");
   832→        assert_eq!(meta.agent_type, "Explore");
   833→        assert!(meta.description.is_some());
   834→    }
   835→
   836→    #[test]
   837→    fn deserialize_agent_meta_no_description() {
   838→        let path = "data/agent-ada8e90bba5770efc.meta.json";
   839→        let file = File::open(path).expect("agent meta file should exist");
   840→        let meta: AgentMeta = serde_json::from_reader(file).expect("agent meta should deserialize");
   841→        assert_eq!(meta.agent_type, "Explore");
   842→        assert!(meta.description.is_none());
   843→    }
   844→
   845→    #[test]
   846→    fn all_variants_covered() {
   847→        let mut seen = HashSet::new();
   848→        for path in TEST_FILES {
   849→            deserialize_file_collecting(path, Some(&mut seen));
   850→        }
   851→        let expected: HashSet<String> =
   852→            Record::all_labels().iter().map(|s| s.to_string()).collect();
   853→        let missing: Vec<_> = expected.difference(&seen).collect();
   854→        assert!(
   855→            missing.is_empty(),
   856→            "Record variants not covered by test data: {missing:?}"
   857→        );
   858→    }
   859→
   860→    // -----------------------------------------------------------------------
   861→    // Error tests — verify known-bad inputs are rejected
   862→    // -----------------------------------------------------------------------
   863→
   864→    const ERR_DATA_DIR: &str = "err-data";
   865→
   866→    fn assert_record_err(filename: &str) {
   867→        let path = format!("{ERR_DATA_DIR}/{filename}");
   868→        let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{path}: {e}"));
   869→        for (i, line) in content.lines().enumerate() {
   870→            if line.trim().is_empty() {
   871→                continue;
   872→            }
   873→            assert!(
   874→                serde_json::from_str::<Record>(line).is_err(),
   875→                "{path}:{} should fail to deserialize but succeeded",
   876→                i + 1,
   877→            );
   878→        }
   879→    }
   880→
   881→    fn assert_meta_err(filename: &str) {
   882→        let path = format!("{ERR_DATA_DIR}/{filename}");
   883→        let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{path}: {e}"));
   884→        assert!(
   885→            serde_json::from_str::<AgentMeta>(&content).is_err(),
   886→            "{path} should fail to deserialize but succeeded",
   887→        );
   888→    }
   889→
   890→    #[test]
   891→    fn err_unknown_variant() {
   892→        assert_record_err("unknown-variant.jsonl");
   893→    }
   894→
   895→    #[test]
   896→    fn err_unknown_field() {
   897→        assert_record_err("unknown-field.jsonl");
   898→    }
   899→
   900→    #[test]
   901→    fn err_wrong_type() {
   902→        assert_record_err("wrong-type.jsonl");
   903→    }
   904→
   905→    #[test]
   906→    fn err_missing_field() {
   907→        assert_record_err("missing-field.jsonl");
   908→    }
   909→
   910→    #[test]
   911→    fn err_bad_json() {
   912→        assert_record_err("bad-json.jsonl");
   913→    }
   914→
   915→    #[test]
   916→    fn err_bad_deser() {
   917→        assert_record_err("bad-deser.jsonl");
   918→    }
   919→
   920→    #[test]
   921→    fn err_bad_meta() {
   922→        assert_meta_err("bad-meta.meta.json");
   923→    }
   924→}

> AGENT

Here's my assessment of json-steroids for this project:

**Compatibility problem**: json-steroids uses its own `#[derive(Json)]` macro — it's not a drop-in for serde. This codebase relies heavily on serde-specific features that json-steroids (v0.1.2) is unlikely to support:

- `#[serde(tag = "type")]` — internally tagged enums (Record, UserContentBlock, AssistantContentBlock)
- `#[serde(untagged)]` — untagged enums (UserContent, ToolResultContent)
- `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` — on every struct
- `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]` — on most structs
- `#[serde(rename = "...")]` — individual field renames

This would be a full rewrite of types.rs (~920 lines), not a dependency swap.

**Is JSON parsing even the bottleneck?** For `ccs-viewer -r data/prgs`:
- Cold: 41s, warm: 12-13s — that 3x cold/warm gap suggests the bottleneck is **filesystem I/O** (directory walking, file reads), not JSON parsing
- The sniff test already skips non-matching files after reading just the first line
- serde_json is already one of the fastest serde-compatible JSON parsers

**More promising speedup avenues** if you want to improve perf:
1. **Parallel file processing** (rayon) — process files across cores
2. **Faster directory walking** (jwalk or ignore crate instead of glob)
3. **mmap** for file reading instead of BufReader
4. **simd-json** — drop-in serde_json replacement, uses SIMD instructions, 2-4x faster parsing with full serde compatibility

simd-json in particular would be nearly zero-effort: just swap `serde_json::from_str` calls to `simd_json::from_str` (it implements serde's Deserialize). No type changes needed.

Want to try simd-json instead, or do you still want to explore json-steroids?

     1→use std::collections::{BTreeMap, HashMap};
     2→use std::fs::File;
     3→use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};
     4→use std::path::PathBuf;
     5→
     6→use clap::Parser;
     7→
     8→use ccs_viewer::types::{AgentMeta, Record};
     9→
    10→#[derive(Parser)]
    11→#[command(
    12→    version,
    13→    about = "Claude Code session JSONL viewer",
    14→    after_help = "\
    15→Output is always in this fixed order regardless of flag order:
    16→  valid (-v) > errors (-e/-E) > skipped (-s) > zero-len (-z) > summary (always last)
    17→
    18→Summary: <total> total files, <valid> valid files with <records> records, <n> zero-len, <n> skipped, <n> errors
    19→  total:    all files found by glob/recursive search
    20→  valid:    files successfully processed (total minus zero-len and skipped)
    21→  records:  total successfully deserialized records (in valid files)
    22→  zero-len: zero-length files
    23→  skipped:  files that failed the first-line sniff test
    24→  errors:   total deserialization failures
    25→
    26→Exit codes:
    27→  0  Success (default, even with deserialization errors)
    28→  1  Tool failure (bad args, can't open file, no files match)
    29→  2  Deserialization errors present (only with --strict)"
    30→)]
    31→struct Cli {
    32→    /// File or directory glob patterns to process
    33→    #[arg(required = true)]
    34→    patterns: Vec<String>,
    35→
    36→    /// Recursively search directories for matching files
    37→    #[arg(short, long)]
    38→    recursive: bool,
    39→
    40→    /// File glob pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: *.jsonl, agent-*.meta.json)
    41→    #[arg(long = "glob")]
    42→    globs: Vec<String>,
    43→
    44→    /// Show valid files (filename + record type counts on two lines)
    45→    #[arg(short, long, help_heading = "Summary detail flags")]
    46→    valid: bool,
    47→
    48→    /// Show error file paths with line numbers
    49→    #[arg(short, long, help_heading = "Summary detail flags")]
    50→    errors: bool,
    51→
    52→    /// Show deduplicated error summary (grouped by message, sorted by count)
    53→    #[arg(short = 'E', long, help_heading = "Summary detail flags")]
    54→    error_summary: bool,
    55→
    56→    /// Show files skipped by the first-line sniff test
    57→    #[arg(short, long, help_heading = "Summary detail flags")]
    58→    skipped: bool,
    59→
    60→    /// Show zero-length files
    61→    #[arg(short, long, help_heading = "Summary detail flags")]
    62→    zero: bool,
    63→
    64→    /// Exit 2 if deserialization errors are present
    65→    #[arg(long)]
    66→    strict: bool,
    67→}
    68→
    69→/// Resolve CLI patterns into a list of file paths.
    70→fn resolve_files(cli: &Cli) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    let mut files = Vec::new();
    72→
    73→    if cli.recursive {
    74→        let file_globs: Vec<&str> = if cli.globs.is_empty() {
    75→            vec!["*.jsonl", "agent-*.meta.json"]
    76→        } else {
    77→            cli.globs.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
    78→        };
    79→
    80→        // Expand positional patterns as directory globs.
    81→        for pattern in &cli.patterns {
    82→            let dirs: Vec<PathBuf> = match glob::glob(pattern) {
    83→                Ok(paths) => paths
    84→                    .filter_map(|p| p.ok())
    85→                    .filter(|p| p.is_dir())
    86→                    .collect(),
    87→                Err(e) => {
    88→                    eprintln!("Bad pattern {pattern:?}: {e}");
    89→                    std::process::exit(1);
    90→                }
    91→            };
    92→            if dirs.is_empty() {
    93→                eprintln!("No directories match {pattern:?}");
    94→                std::process::exit(1);
    95→            }
    96→            for dir in &dirs {
    97→                for fg in &file_globs {
    98→                    let full = format!("{}/**/{fg}", dir.display());
    99→                    match glob::glob(&full) {
   100→                        Ok(paths) => {
   101→                            for entry in paths.flatten() {
   102→                                if entry.is_file() {
   103→                                    files.push(entry);
   104→                                }
   105→                            }
   106→                        }
   107→                        Err(e) => {
   108→                            eprintln!("Bad glob {full:?}: {e}");
   109→                            std::process::exit(1);
   110→                        }
   111→                    }
   112→                }
   113→            }
   114→        }
   115→    } else {
   116→        let file_globs: Vec<&str> = if cli.globs.is_empty() {
   117→            vec!["*.jsonl", "agent-*.meta.json"]
   118→        } else {
   119→            cli.globs.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
   120→        };
   121→
   122→        for pattern in &cli.patterns {
   123→            // Check if pattern is a literal directory path first.
   124→            let path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
   125→            if path.is_dir() {
   126→                // Directory: expand file globs inside it (non-recursive).
   127→                for fg in &file_globs {
   128→                    let full = format!("{}/{fg}", path.display());
   129→                    match glob::glob(&full) {
   130→                        Ok(paths) => {
   131→                            for entry in paths.flatten() {
   132→                                if entry.is_file() {
   133→                                    files.push(entry);
   134→                                }
   135→                            }
   136→                        }
   137→                        Err(e) => {
   138→                            eprintln!("Bad glob {full:?}: {e}");
   139→                            std::process::exit(1);
   140→                        }
   141→                    }
   142→                }
   143→            } else {
   144→                // Treat as file glob pattern.
   145→                match glob::glob(pattern) {
   146→                    Ok(paths) => {
   147→                        let mut matched = Vec::new();
   148→                        for entry in paths.flatten() {
   149→                            if entry.is_file() {
   150→                                matched.push(entry);
   151→                            } else if entry.is_dir() {
   152→                                // Glob matched a directory: expand file
   153→                                // globs inside it (non-recursive).
   154→                                for fg in &file_globs {
   155→                                    let full = format!("{}/{fg}", entry.display());
   156→                                    if let Ok(inner) = glob::glob(&full) {
   157→                                        for f in inner.flatten() {
   158→                                            if f.is_file() {
   159→                                                matched.push(f);
   160→                                            }
   161→                                        }
   162→                                    }
   163→                                }
   164→                            }
   165→                        }
   166→                        if matched.is_empty() {
   167→                            eprintln!("No files match {pattern:?}");
   168→                            std::process::exit(1);
   169→                        }
   170→                        files.extend(matched);
   171→                    }
   172→                    Err(e) => {
   173→                        eprintln!("Bad pattern {pattern:?}: {e}");
   174→                        std::process::exit(1);
   175→                    }
   176→                }
   177→            }
   178→        }
   179→    }
   180→
   181→    files.sort();
   182→    files.dedup();
   183→    files
   184→}
   185→
   186→/// Key for grouping errors: the serde error message + record type.
   187→#[derive(Hash, Eq, PartialEq)]
   188→struct ErrorKey {
   189→    message: String,
   190→    record_type: String,
   191→}
   192→
   193→/// A single file:line occurrence of an error.
   194→struct ErrorHit {
   195→    path: String,
   196→    line: usize,
   197→}
   198→
   199→/// Tracks one group of identical errors across files.
   200→struct ErrorGroup {
   201→    count: usize,
   202→    /// How many distinct files contain this error.
   203→    file_count: usize,
   204→    /// Track which files we've seen (by index) to count distinct files.
   205→    seen_files: Vec<usize>,
   206→    /// All file:line occurrences (one per distinct file).
   207→    hits: Vec<ErrorHit>,
   208→}
   209→
   210→fn main() {
   211→    let cli = Cli::parse();
   212→    let files = resolve_files(&cli);
   213→
   214→    if files.is_empty() {
   215→        eprintln!("No files to process");
   216→        std::process::exit(1);
   217→    }
   218→
   219→    let mut total_records: usize = 0;
   220→    let mut total_errors: usize = 0;
   221→    let mut total_skipped: usize = 0;
   222→    let mut total_empty: usize = 0;
   223→    let mut skipped_files: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   224→    let mut empty_files: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   225→    let mut error_groups: HashMap<ErrorKey, ErrorGroup> = HashMap::new();
   226→    let mut valid_header_printed = false;
   227→
   228→    for (file_idx, path) in files.iter().enumerate() {
   229→        let file = File::open(path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
   230→            eprintln!("Error opening {}: {e}", path.display());
   231→            std::process::exit(1);
   232→        });
   233→
   234→        let filename = path
   235→            .file_name()
   236→            .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   237→            .unwrap_or_else(|| path.display().to_string());
   238→
   239→        // Handle .meta.json files as single-object JSON (not JSONL).
   240→        if filename.ends_with(".meta.json") {
   241→            let metadata = file.metadata().ok();
   242→            if metadata.as_ref().is_some_and(|m| m.len() == 0) {
   243→                total_empty += 1;
   244→                if cli.zero {
   245→                    empty_files.push(path.display().to_string());
   246→                }
   247→                continue;
   248→            }
   249→            let result: Result<AgentMeta, _> = serde_json::from_reader(file);
   250→            match result {
   251→                Ok(_meta) => {
   252→                    total_records += 1;
   253→                    if cli.valid {
   254→                        if !valid_header_printed {
   255→                            println!("Valid:");
   256→                            valid_header_printed = true;
   257→                        }
   258→                        println!("  {}", path.display());
   259→                        println!("    agent-meta (ok)");
   260→                    }
   261→                }
   262→                Err(e) => {
   263→                    total_errors += 1;
   264→                    let key = ErrorKey {
   265→                        message: format!("{e}"),
   266→                        record_type: "agent-meta".to_string(),
   267→                    };
   268→                    let group = error_groups.entry(key).or_insert_with(|| ErrorGroup {
   269→                        count: 0,
   270→                        file_count: 0,
   271→                        seen_files: Vec::new(),
   272→                        hits: Vec::new(),
   273→                    });
   274→                    group.count += 1;
   275→                    if !group.seen_files.contains(&file_idx) {
   276→                        group.seen_files.push(file_idx);
   277→                        group.file_count += 1;
   278→                        group.hits.push(ErrorHit {
   279→                            path: path.display().to_string(),
   280→                            line: 1,
   281→                        });
   282→                    }
   283→                    if cli.valid {
   284→                        if !valid_header_printed {
   285→                            println!("Valid:");
   286→                            valid_header_printed = true;
   287→                        }
   288→                        println!("  {}", path.display());
   289→                        println!("    agent-meta (error)");
   290→                    }
   291→                }
   292→            }
   293→            continue;
   294→        }
   295→
   296→        let mut reader = BufReader::new(file);
   297→
   298→        // Check for empty files before the sniff test.
   299→        let mut first_line = String::new();
   300→        if reader.read_line(&mut first_line).unwrap_or(0) == 0 {
   301→            total_empty += 1;
   302→            if cli.zero {
   303→                empty_files.push(path.display().to_string());
   304→            }
   305→            continue;
   306→        }
   307→
   308→        // First-line sniff test: skip .jsonl files that don't look like
   309→        // Claude Code sessions. CCS files start with {"type": or {"parentUuid":.
   310→        let trimmed = first_line.trim();
   311→        if !trimmed.starts_with("{\"type\":") && !trimmed.starts_with("{\"parentUuid\":") {
   312→            total_skipped += 1;
   313→            if cli.skipped {
   314→                skipped_files.push(path.display().to_string());
   315→            }
   316→            continue;
   317→        }
   318→
   319→        // Process lines starting from the first line we already read.
   320→        let mut counts: BTreeMap<&str, usize> = BTreeMap::new();
   321→        let mut file_errors: usize = 0;
   322→
   323→        // Process first line, then remaining lines.
   324→        let remaining_lines = reader.lines().map(|l| l.unwrap_or_default());
   325→        let all_lines = std::iter::once(first_line.trim_end().to_string()).chain(remaining_lines);
   326→
   327→        for (i, line) in all_lines.enumerate() {
   328→            if line.trim().is_empty() {
   329→                continue;
   330→            }
   331→            match serde_json::from_str::<Record>(&line) {
   332→                Ok(record) => {
   333→                    *counts.entry(record.label()).or_insert(0) += 1;
   334→                }
   335→                Err(e) => {
   336→                    file_errors += 1;
   337→                    let record_type = serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&line)
   338→                        .ok()
   339→                        .and_then(|v| v.get("type")?.as_str().map(String::from))
   340→                        .unwrap_or_else(|| "unknown".to_string());
   341→
   342→                    let key = ErrorKey {
   343→                        message: format!("{e}"),
   344→                        record_type,
   345→                    };
   346→                    let group = error_groups.entry(key).or_insert_with(|| ErrorGroup {
   347→                        count: 0,
   348→                        file_count: 0,
   349→                        seen_files: Vec::new(),
   350→                        hits: Vec::new(),
   351→                    });
   352→                    group.count += 1;
   353→                    if !group.seen_files.contains(&file_idx) {
   354→                        group.seen_files.push(file_idx);
   355→                        group.file_count += 1;
   356→                        group.hits.push(ErrorHit {
   357→                            path: path.display().to_string(),
   358→                            line: i + 1,
   359→                        });
   360→                    }
   361→                }
   362→            }
   363→        }
   364→
   365→        let file_total: usize = counts.values().sum();
   366→        total_records += file_total;
   367→        total_errors += file_errors;
   368→
   369→        if cli.valid {
   370→            if !valid_header_printed {
   371→                println!("Valid:");
   372→                valid_header_printed = true;
   373→            }
   374→            let mut parts = vec![
   375→                format!("errors: {file_errors}"),
   376→                format!("records: {file_total}"),
   377→            ];
   378→            for (label, count) in &counts {
   379→                parts.push(format!("{label}: {count}"));
   380→            }
   381→            println!("  {}", path.display());
   382→            println!("    {}", parts.join(", "));
   383→        }
   384→    }
   385→
   386→    let file_count = files.len();
   387→    let processed = file_count - total_skipped - total_empty;
   388→
   389→    if valid_header_printed {
   390→        println!();
   391→    }
   392→
   393→    let show_errors = cli.errors || cli.error_summary;
   394→    if show_errors && !error_groups.is_empty() {
   395→        let mut groups: Vec<_> = error_groups.iter().collect();
   396→        groups.sort_by(|a, b| b.1.count.cmp(&a.1.count));
   397→
   398→        if cli.error_summary && cli.errors {
   399→            // -e -E combined: grouped summary with all paths nested
   400→            println!("Error summary:");
   401→            for (key, group) in &groups {
   402→                println!("  {}x {} in {}", group.count, key.message, key.record_type,);
   403→                for hit in &group.hits {
   404→                    println!("    {}:{}", hit.path, hit.line);
   405→                }
   406→            }
   407→            println!();
   408→        } else if cli.error_summary {
   409→            // -E only: grouped summary, two lines per group
   410→            println!("Error summary:");
   411→            for (key, group) in &groups {
   412→                let first = &group.hits[0];
   413→                println!("  {}x {} in {}", group.count, key.message, key.record_type,);
   414→                println!(
   415→                    "    {} file(s), first: {}:{}",
   416→                    group.file_count, first.path, first.line,
   417→                );
   418→            }
   419→            println!();
   420→        } else if cli.errors {
   421→            // -e only: flat list of all error file:line paths
   422→            println!("Errors:");
   423→            let mut all_hits: Vec<_> = error_groups.values().flat_map(|g| g.hits.iter()).collect();
   424→            all_hits.sort_by(|a, b| a.path.cmp(&b.path).then(a.line.cmp(&b.line)));
   425→            for hit in all_hits {
   426→                println!("  {}:{}", hit.path, hit.line);
   427→            }
   428→            println!();
   429→        }
   430→    }
   431→
   432→    if cli.skipped && !skipped_files.is_empty() {
   433→        println!("Skipped:");
   434→        for f in &skipped_files {
   435→            println!("  {f}");
   436→        }
   437→        println!();
   438→    }
   439→
   440→    if cli.zero && !empty_files.is_empty() {
   441→        println!("Zero-len:");
   442→        for f in &empty_files {
   443→            println!("  {f}");
   444→        }
   445→        println!();
   446→    }
   447→
   448→    let total_files = processed + total_skipped + total_empty;
   449→    println!(
   450→        "Summary: {total_files} total files, {processed} valid files with {total_records} records, {total_empty} zero-len, {total_skipped} skipped, {total_errors} errors"
   451→    );
   452→
   453→    if cli.strict && total_errors > 0 {
   454→        std::process::exit(2);
   455→    }
   456→}
   457→

Right — simd-json mutates the input buffer even on error. The error fallback needs the original string. I'll extract the type field with a simple string search instead of re-parsing, since we already know the line starts with `{"type":` from the sniff test:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_012o6ARmBrw1yLPNromfPp1W
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs",
  "old_string": "        for (i, mut line) in all_lines.enumerate() {\n            if line.trim().is_empty() {\n                continue;\n            }\n            match simd_json::serde::from_str::<Record>(&mut line) {\n                Ok(record) => {\n                    *counts.entry(record.label()).or_insert(0) += 1;\n                }\n                Err(e) => {\n                    file_errors += 1;\n                    let record_type = serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&line)\n                        .ok()\n                        .and_then(|v| v.get(\"type\")?.as_str().map(String::from))\n                        .unwrap_or_else(|| \"unknown\".to_string());",
  "new_string": "        for (i, mut line) in all_lines.enumerate() {\n            if line.trim().is_empty() {\n                continue;\n            }\n            // Extract type field before simd-json mutates the buffer.\n            let record_type_hint = extract_type_field(&line);\n            match simd_json::serde::from_str::<Record>(&mut line) {\n                Ok(record) => {\n                    *counts.entry(record.label()).or_insert(0) += 1;\n                }\n                Err(e) => {\n                    file_errors += 1;\n                    let record_type =\n                        record_type_hint.unwrap_or_else(|| \"unknown\".to_string());"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs",

  "new_string": "/// Extract the `\"type\"` value from a JSON line without full parsing.\n/// Returns `None` if the pattern `\"type\":\"<value>\"` is not found.\nfn extract_type_field(line: &str) -> Option<String> {\n    let marker = \"\\\"type\\\":\\\"\";\n    let start = line.find(marker)? + marker.len();\n    let end = start + line[start..].find('\"')?;\n    Some(line[start..end].to_string())\n}\n\nfn main() {"
}

> AGENT

Right — simd-json's `from_str` is `unsafe` because it mutates the input. Need to wrap in `unsafe` blocks:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_017NCZTSz9s8HhHcpWWbERsZ
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/ccs-viewer-by-cc/src/main.rs",
  "old_string": "            let mut contents = std::io::read_to_string(file).unwrap_or_default();\n            let result: Result<AgentMeta, _> = simd_json::serde::from_str(&mut contents);",
  "new_string": "            let mut contents = std::io::read_to_string(file).unwrap_or_default();\n            // SAFETY: simd-json mutates the buffer in-place for zero-copy parsing;\n            // `contents` is an owned String not shared elsewhere.\n            let result: Result<AgentMeta, _> =\n                unsafe { simd_json::serde::from_str(&mut contents) };"
}
```

> AGENT

Results: **~13s warm** — essentially the same as the serde_json baseline (12-13s). No measurable improvement.

This confirms the bottleneck is **not JSON parsing**. Look at the breakdown:
- **user time: ~5.7s** (CPU doing actual work, including JSON parsing)
- **sys time: ~7.3s** (kernel time — filesystem/IO operations)

Over half the wall time is kernel I/O — directory walking with glob and reading 902 files. simd-json can't help with that.

The real wins would come from:
1. **Parallel file processing** (rayon) — use all cores for both I/O and parsing
2. **Faster directory walking** (jwalk/ignore crate instead of glob) — these use parallel traversal

Want to pursue either of those, or shelve the simd-json change? It's not hurting anything but adds 28 dependencies for no gain.

> DEVELOPER

let me flush the cache, what's the sudo command?

> AGENT

```
sudo sh -c 'echo 3 > /proc/sys/vm/drop_caches'
```

> DEVELOPER

id: toolu_016xqvcHULmhwqsoauBLs8Eh
```
     1→# ccs-viewer
     2→
     3→A CLI tool for viewing and validating Claude Code session files
     4→(JSONL and agent meta.json). Written in Rust, built by Claude Code.
     5→
     6→This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
     7→a bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal
     8→is that this main repo contains the "what", while the partner
     9→bot repo contains "why" and "how". The key to the convention
    10→is each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there
    11→is a coherent story of the development of the project across time.
    12→
    13→The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
    14→which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
    15→first test bed.
    16→
    17→## CLI Usage
    18→
    19→```
    20→ccs-viewer [OPTIONS] <PATTERNS>...
    21→```
    22→
    23→By default, only the summary line is printed. Use flags for more detail.
    24→
    25→### Options
    26→
    27→| Flag | Short | Description |
    28→|------|-------|-------------|
    29→| `--recursive` | `-r` | Treat positional args as directories, search recursively |
    30→| `--glob <PAT>` | | File pattern for recursive mode (repeatable, default: `*.jsonl`, `agent-*.meta.json`) |
    31→| `--strict` | | Exit 2 if deserialization errors are present |
    32→| `--version` | `-V` | Print version |
    33→| `--help` | `-h` | Print help |
    34→
    35→**Summary detail flags** (output order is fixed regardless of flag order):
    36→
    37→| Flag | Short | Description |
    38→|------|-------|-------------|
    39→| `--valid` | `-v` | Show valid files (path + record type counts on two lines) |
    40→| `--errors` | `-e` | Show error file paths with line numbers (or nest under groups with `-E`) |
    41→| `--error-summary` | `-E` | Show deduplicated error summary (grouped by message, sorted by count) |
    42→| `-e -E` | | Combined: grouped summary with all file paths nested under each group |
    43→| `--skipped` | `-s` | Show files that failed the first-line sniff test |
    44→| `--zero` | `-z` | Show zero-length files |
    45→
    46→### Positional arguments
    47→
    48→Without `-r`: file glob patterns expanded by the program.
    49→With `-r`: directory paths (or directory globs) to search recursively.
    50→
    51→### Examples
    52→
    53→```bash
    54→# Summary only (default)
    55→ccs-viewer "data/*.jsonl"
    56→
    57→# Per-file list + summary
    58→ccs-viewer -v "data/*.jsonl"
    59→
    60→# Summary + error details
    61→ccs-viewer -e "data/*.jsonl"
    62→
    63→# Recursive search in a directory
    64→ccs-viewer -r .claude
    65→
    66→# Recursive with custom file patterns
    67→ccs-viewer -r --glob "*.jsonl" --glob "*.json" .claude
    68→
    69→# Multiple directories
    70→ccs-viewer -r .claude ../vc-x1/.claude
    71→
    72→# Directory glob
    73→ccs-viewer -r "../*/.claude"
    74→
    75→# All detail flags
    76→ccs-viewer -r -v -e -s -z .claude
    77→
    78→# Pick up ALL files (including non-jsonl) — handy for spotting strays
    79→ccs-viewer -r -e -E -s -z --glob '*' ./data ./err-data
    80→
    81→# Strict mode for CI
    82→ccs-viewer -r --strict .claude
    83→```
    84→
    85→### Example output
    86→
    87→```
    88→$ ccs-viewer -E -r .claude
    89→Error summary:
    90→  2x unknown variant `summary` at line 1 column 17 in summary
    91→    2 file(s), first: abc.jsonl:1
    92→  1x invalid type: null, expected a string in assistant
    93→    1 file(s), first: def.jsonl:1
    94→
    95→Summary: 46 total files, 41 valid files with 1523 records, 2 zero-len, 3 skipped, 3 errors
    96→```
    97→
    98→When `-e` and `-E` are combined, file paths are nested under each group:
    99→
   100→```
   101→$ ccs-viewer -e -E -r .claude
   102→Error summary:
   103→  2x unknown variant `summary` at line 1 column 17 in summary
   104→    abc.jsonl:1
   105→    xyz.jsonl:5
   106→  1x invalid type: null, expected a string in assistant
   107→    def.jsonl:1
   108→
   109→Summary: 46 total files, 41 valid files with 1523 records, 2 zero-len, 3 skipped, 3 errors
   110→```
   111→
   112→Using `--glob '*'` to pick up all files (non-matching files appear as skipped):
   113→
   114→```
   115→$ ccs-viewer -e -E -s -z -r --glob '*' ./data ./err-data
   116→Error summary:
   117→  1x invalid type: string "not-a-bool", expected a boolean in user
   118→    err-data/bad-deser.jsonl:1
   119→  1x missing field `sessionId` in last-prompt
   120→    err-data/missing-field.jsonl:1
   121→  1x unknown variant `bogus`, expected one of `file-history-snapshot`, `user`, `assistant`, `progress`, `last-prompt`, `queue-operation`, `system`, `custom-title`, `agent-name`, `summary` at line 1 column 15 in bogus
   122→    err-data/unknown-variant.jsonl:1
   123→  1x unknown field `badField`, expected `agentType` or `description` at line 1 column 34 in agent-meta
   124→    err-data/bad-meta.meta.json:1
   125→  1x unknown field `extraField`, expected `lastPrompt` or `sessionId` in last-prompt
   126→    err-data/unknown-field.jsonl:1
   127→  1x invalid type: integer `123`, expected a string in last-prompt
   128→    err-data/wrong-type.jsonl:1
   129→
   130→Skipped:
   131→  err-data/bad-json.jsonl
   132→  err-data/hello.txt
   133→  err-data/non-json.json
   134→
   135→Zero-len:
   136→  data/agent-0-len.meta.json
   137→
   138→Summary: 18 total files, 14 valid files with 1036 records, 1 zero-len, 3 skipped, 6 errors
   139→```
   140→
   141→### Output
   142→
   143→Output is always in this fixed order regardless of flag order:
   144→valid files (if `-v`) → errors (if `-e`/`-E`) → skipped files (if `-s`)
   145→→ zero-len files (if `-z`) → **summary** (always last).
   146→
   147→The **summary line** is always printed and always last:
   148→
   149→```
   150→Summary: <total> total files, <valid> valid files with <records> records, <n> zero-len, <n> skipped, <n> errors
   151→```
   152→
   153→| Field | Meaning |
   154→|-------|---------|
   155→| total | All files found by glob/recursive search |
   156→| valid | Files successfully processed (total minus zero-len and skipped) |
   157→| records | Total successfully deserialized records (in valid files) |
   158→| zero-len | Zero-length files |
   159→| skipped | Files that failed the first-line sniff test |
   160→| errors | Total deserialization failures |
   161→
   162→### Exit codes
   163→
   164→- 0: success (default, even with deserialization errors)
   165→- 1: tool failure (bad args, can't open file, no files match)
   166→- 2: deserialization errors present (only with `--strict`)
   167→
   168→### First-line sniff test
   169→
   170→JSONL files are checked before processing: if the first line doesn't
   171→start with `{"type":` or `{"parentUuid":`, the file is skipped. This
   172→filters out non-Claude-Code JSONL files (benchmark logs, etc.) without
   173→relying on filename patterns. Use `-s` to see which files were skipped.
   174→
   175→## jj Tips for Git Users
   176→
   177→See [Steve Klabnik](https://github.com/steveklabnik)
   178→[Jujutsu-tutorial](https://steveklabnik.github.io/jujutsu-tutorial)
   179→and [jj docs](https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/).
   180→
   181→### Initial Commit for a repo
   182→
   183→Create create directory add files.
   184→
   185→Minimal commands to push 
   186→
   187→```
   188→jj git init .
   189→jj describe
   190→jj git remote add origin git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
   191→jj bookmark create main -r @
   192→jj bookmark track main --remote=origin
   193→jj git push
   194→```
   195→
   196→### Push a change to main
   197→
   198→Assuming that this is to be push to main you
   199→set the bookmark to the appropriate commit and
   200→then just push:
   201→
   202→```
   203→jj bookmark set main -r @
   204→jj git push
   205→```
   206→
   207→Complete example:
   208→```
   209→wink@3900x 26-03-13T17:26:21.177Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-template-x1 ((jj/keep/1a79f803025f75fb557a7b6f9d29e3dbee6a1724))
   210→$ vi README.md 
   211→wink@3900x 26-03-13T17:28:08.833Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-template-x1 ((jj/keep/1a79f803025f75fb557a7b6f9d29e3dbee6a1724))

   234→
   235→### Example of modifying an existing commit and "force" push
   236→
   237→Tweak a commit and push it using `jj edit` then "force" push:
   238→
   239→Minimum steps changing xx but it could be any commit on main
   240→or other bookmark/branch the last step repositions @ so @- is main:
   241→
   242→```
   243→jj edit -r xxx --ignore-immutable
   244→<Modify the commit such as, `jj describe or `vi README.md`>
   245→jj git push --bookmark main
   246→jj new main
   247→```
   248→

   330→
   331→### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
   332→
   333→If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
   334→tools like `gitk --all`.
   335→
   336→jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
   337→When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
   338→jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
   339→undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
   340→shows the current version of each change.
   341→
   342→### Useful commands
   343→
   344→| Command | Description |
   345→|---------|-------------|
   346→| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
   347→| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
   348→| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
   349→| `jj st | Show the status of the Working and Parent commits |
   350→| `jj st -r <chid> | Status of the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
   351→| `jj show | Show the Working commit, -r @ |
   352→| `jj show -r <chid> | Show the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
   353→| `jj evolog -r <chid>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
   354→| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
   355→
   356→
   357→In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
   358→the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
   359→
   360→## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
   361→
   362→Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
   363→to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
   364→(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
   365→and jj changeID:
   366→
   367→```
   368→ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
   369→ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
   370→```
   371→
   372→Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
   373→Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
   374→within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
   375→
   376→For full details see:
   377→- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
   378→  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
   379→  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
   380→  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
   381→

     3→This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
     4→Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
     5→

    27→
    28→## jj tips
    29→
    30→For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
    31→
    32→```
    33→## Chores format
    34→
    35→Filename: "Chores-XX-.md"
    36→example: chores-01.md
    37→
    38→Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
    39→example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
    40→
    41→Example chore file:
    42→```
    43→# Chores-01.md
    44→ 
    45→General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
    46→more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
    47→how and why of a particular chore.
    48→
    49→## Do something (1.3.1)
    50→
    51→Describe something
    52→```
    53→
    54→## Versioning during development
    55→
    56→This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
    57→git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
    58→we go.
    59→
    60→Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
    61→
    62→- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
    63→  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
    64→- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
    65→  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
    66→
    67→The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
    68→
    69→For multi-step:
    70→1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
    71→2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
    72→3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
    73→
    74→The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
    75→prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
    76→commits were exploratory vs final.
    77→
    78→## Todo format
    79→
    80→Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
    81→short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
    82→references.
    83→
    84→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
    85→In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
    86→is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
    87→
    88→Examples:
    89→
    90→# Todo
    91→- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
    92→- Fix bug Y [1]
    93→
    94→# Done
    95→- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
    96→
    97→[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
    98→
```

    10→## Have claude code design a claude-code session viewer
    11→
    12→  In `hwr.claude/` is a .jsonl file with a set of json lines representing me asking
    13→  claude-code to "reqaquaint" (sp: reaquaint) itself with a trivial app created
    14→  using, `cargo new hwr` and `hwr.claude/` is the directory `~/.claude/projects/<path>/`.
    15→
    16→  Medium term want to convert the jsonl lines into a the "conversation".
    17→  In the short term I'd like to create the rust structs that each represent
    18→  the information, I don't want to process it I just want to be able to
    19→  use serde to deserialize the data in the file into the set of rust structs.
    20→  
    21→  I think each line should destruct into a single struct, but I suspect
    22→  this "super" struct is composed of several substructures and those
    23→  should be defined separately and used but the "super" struct.
    24→  
    25→  In the end we'll have an app that can read a full conversation with
    26→  claude-code and display it. Long term this capability will be combined
    27→  with the app repo and we'll create a coherent story of why, how and what
    28→  for the app.
    29→
    30→  Take a look at the file and let me know what you think
    31→
    32→## Define serde structs for JSONL deserialization (0.1.0)
    33→
    34→  Created `src/types.rs` with serde structs to deserialize all 5 JSONL record
    35→  types from Claude Code session files:
    36→
    37→  - `Record` — top-level enum, internally tagged on `"type"` field

```json
{
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json, sys\nfrom collections import Counter\n\n# Analyze the conversation structure of a session\nwith open('data/997afb98-c6aa-4f4a-92ac-a841e040414b.jsonl') as f:\n    records = [json.loads(l) for l in f if l.strip()]\n\nprint(f'Total records: {len(records)}')\nprint()\n\n# Record type distribution\ntypes = Counter(r.get('type','?') for r in records)\nfor t, c in types.most_common():\n    print(f'  {t}: {c}')\n\nprint()\n\n# Show the conversation flow (first 20 records)\nprint('Conversation flow (first 20):')\nfor i, r in enumerate(records[:20]):\n    rtype = r.get('type','?')\n    if rtype == 'user':\n        content = r['message']['content']\n        if isinstance(content, str):\n            print(f'  [{i}] USER: {content[:80]}')\n        else:\n            block_types = [b['type'] for b in content]\n            print(f'  [{i}] USER: [{\\\" + \\\".join(block_types)}]')\n    elif rtype == 'assistant':\n        blocks = r['message']['content']\n        block_types = [b['type'] for b in blocks]\n        text_preview = ''\n        for b in blocks:\n            if b['type'] == 'text':\n                text_preview = b['text'][:60]\n                break\n            elif b['type'] == 'tool_use':\n                text_preview = f'tool:{b[\\\"name\\\"]}'\n                break\n        print(f'  [{i}] ASSISTANT [{\\\" + \\\".join(block_types)}]: {text_preview}')\n    elif rtype == 'progress':\n        dtype = r['data']['type']\n        print(f'  [{i}] PROGRESS: {dtype}')\n    elif rtype == 'system':\n        sub = r.get('subtype','?')\n        print(f'  [{i}] SYSTEM: {sub}')\n    else:\n        print(f'  [{i}] {rtype}')\n\"",
  "description": "Analyze conversation structure of a larger session"
}

> AGENT

Good, now I have a clear picture. Here's where things stand and the design space:

---

## What we have

A **validation/summary** tool: parses JSONL, counts records, reports errors. The types are fully modeled in `types.rs` — 10 record variants with all fields typed.

## What "viewing" means

A session is a **conversation tree**:
- `user` → `assistant` exchanges linked by `parentUuid`
- Assistant responses contain **thinking**, **text**, and **tool_use** blocks
- `progress` records interleave (tool execution status — agent spawns, commands, searches)
- `system` records mark API errors, retries, context compaction
- `file-history-snapshot` records track file state at each point
- Agents create sub-conversations (linked by `agentId`)

A 461-record session has 133 user + 182 assistant messages with 95 progress events woven in. The "story" is buried in there.

## Incremental path (simple → rich)

### Phase 1: CLI transcript mode (stay in current binary)
Add a `--show` / `--transcript` flag that renders a readable conversation:
- Color-coded roles (user=green, assistant=blue, system=yellow)
- Show text content inline, summarize tool_use as one-liners (`[tool: Read src/main.rs]`)
- Collapse or hide thinking blocks by default (`--thinking` to expand)
- Skip progress/file-history-snapshot by default (`--all` to include)
- Pipe through a pager automatically when stdout is a tty

This gets you reading sessions immediately with zero new deps (just ANSI codes).

### Phase 2: TUI (new binary or mode)
**ratatui** is the clear choice in Rust — actively maintained, rich widget set, used by major tools (gitui, bottom, lazygit-style apps). Key layout:

```
┌─ Sessions ──────┬─ Conversation ──────────────────────────┐
│ session-31ba... │ [USER] reqaquaint                       │
│ session-997a... │                                         │
│ session-092d... │ [ASSISTANT] Let me look at the project  │
│                 │   [tool: Read Cargo.toml]               │
│                 │   [tool: Glob **/*.rs]                  │
│                 │                                         │
│                 │ [USER] [tool_result: 14 lines]          │
│                 │                                         │
│                 │ [ASSISTANT] This is a Rust project...   │
├─────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Agents          │ Metadata / Details                      │
│  Explore (3)    │ model: claude-opus-4-6                  │
│  Plan (1)       │ tokens: 1234 in / 567 out              │
│                 │ timestamp: 2026-03-21T21:19:13Z         │
└─────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- Left: session/file browser + agent tree
- Main: scrollable conversation with expand/collapse per block
- Bottom: metadata panel for selected record
- Keybinds: j/k scroll, Enter expand, tab switch panes, / search

### Phase 3: The "story" view (the long-term vision)
Cross-reference sessions with git history via ochid trailers:
- Show which conversation produced which commits
- Timeline view: code changes interleaved with the conversations that drove them
- Diff view: see what the assistant actually changed alongside the discussion

## Options for the GUI path (later)

| Framework | Style | Pros | Cons |
|-----------|-------|------|------|
| **ratatui** | Terminal | Zero runtime deps, fast, works over SSH | No images, limited layout |
| **egui** | Immediate-mode GUI | Very easy to prototype, cross-platform | Not native-looking |
| **iced** | Elm-style GUI | Native feel, good for complex layouts | Steeper learning curve |
| **tauri** | Web frontend + Rust backend | Full HTML/CSS, richest rendering | Heavyweight, needs JS |

My recommendation: **Phase 1 first** (CLI transcript) — it's a small delta from where we are, immediately useful, and forces us to figure out the right data flow (filtering, ordering, collapsing) before investing in UI framework choices. Then **Phase 2 with ratatui** since TUI fits the session-browsing use case well and keeps the tool lightweight.

Want to dig deeper into any of these phases, or shall we start sketching Phase 1?

> AGENT

Good plan. For the 1-3 types to start with, I'd pick:

1. **user** - show `message.content` (plain text string or summarize tool_result blocks as `[tool_result: <id>]`)
2. **assistant** - show `text` blocks inline, summarize `tool_use` as `[tool: Name]`, hide `thinking` by default

These two give you the conversation skeleton. Everything else (progress, system, file-history-snapshot) is detail that can layer in later. Starting with just user+assistant text also keeps the first implementation very small — maybe a `--show` flag that loops through records and prints the readable bits.

For the two commits:

**0.15.0** (single-step): bump version, chores-02.md, update todo — docs only, run the checklist for the version bump.

**0.16.0-dev0** (multi-step start): bump version, write the Phase 1 plan into chores-02.md as a new section, update todo.

Sound right?

    75→
    76→- Left: session/file browser + agent tree
    77→- Main: scrollable conversation with expand/collapse per block
    78→- Bottom: metadata panel for selected record
    79→- Keybinds: j/k scroll, Enter expand, tab switch panes, / search
    80→
    81→### Phase 3: the "story" view
    82→
    83→Cross-reference sessions with git history via ochid trailers:
    84→
    85→- Show which conversation produced which commits
    86→- Timeline view: code changes interleaved with the conversations
    87→  that drove them
    88→- Diff view: see what the assistant actually changed alongside
    89→  the discussion
    90→
    91→### GUI framework options (for later)
    92→
    93→| Framework   | Style                       | Pros                                            | Cons                        |
    94→|-------------|-----------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|
    95→| **ratatui** | Terminal                    | Zero runtime deps, fast, works over SSH         | No images, limited layout   |
    96→| **egui**    | Immediate-mode GUI          | Very easy to prototype, cross-platform          | Not native-looking          |
    97→| **iced**    | Elm-style GUI               | Native feel, good for complex layouts           | Steeper learning curve      |
    98→| **tauri**   | Web frontend + Rust backend | Full HTML/CSS, richest rendering                | Heavyweight, needs JS       |
    99→
   100→Recommendation: Phase 1 first (immediately useful, small delta),
   101→then Phase 2 with ratatui (TUI fits session-browsing well, keeps
   102→the tool lightweight). GUI frameworks for Phase 3+ if needed.
   103→

  "new_string": "Recommendation: Phase 1 first (immediately useful, small delta),\nthen Phase 2 with ratatui (TUI fits session-browsing well, keeps\nthe tool lightweight). GUI frameworks for Phase 3+ if needed.\n\n## Phase 1 plan: CLI transcript (0.16.0)\n\nStart small: display just `user` and `assistant` records from a\nsingle session file. This is the minimum needed to read a\nconversation.\n\n### Scope\n\nAdd a `--show` flag. When given, instead of the validation summary,\nprint a readable transcript of the conversation. Operates on a\nsingle file (first positional arg; error if multiple files with\n`--show`).\n\n### Record types displayed\n\n1. **user** — show `message.content`:\n   - If plain text string: print it directly\n   - If content blocks: print `text` blocks inline, summarize\n     `tool_result` blocks as `[tool_result]`\n\n2. **assistant** — show `message.content` blocks:\n   - `text` blocks: print inline\n   - `tool_use` blocks: one-liner `[tool: <name>]`\n   - `thinking` blocks: skip (future flag `--thinking` to show)\n\nAll other record types (progress, system, file-history-snapshot,\nqueue-operation, custom-title, agent-name, summary) are silently\nskipped in this first pass.\n\n### Output format\n\nSimple, no color in the first cut. Color can come in a follow-up.\n\n```\n--- user ---\nreqaquaint\n\n--- assistant ---\nLet me look at the project structure.\n[tool: Read Cargo.toml]\n[tool: Glob **/*.rs]\n\n--- user ---\n[tool_result]\n\n--- assistant ---\nThis is a Rust project with the following structure...\n```\n\n### Implementation steps\n\n1. Add `--show` flag to Cli struct (conflicts with `-v`, `-e`, etc.)\n2. In main, when `--show` is set: parse the file, iterate records,\n   print user/assistant content per the format above\n3. Add a test with existing test data verifying output shape\n\n### What this defers\n\n- Color / ANSI formatting\n- Thinking block display\n- Progress / system / other record types\n- Pager integration\n- Multi-file support with `--show`"
}

<system-reminder>This memory is 3 days old. Memories are point-in-time observations, not live state — claims about code behavior or file:line citations may be outdated. Verify against current code before asserting as fact.</system-reminder>
     1→# Memory
     2→
     3→## User Preferences
     4→
     5→- **Commit approval**: Always present the full commit commands to the user for physical review BEFORE executing them. Do not run jj commit / git commit until the user explicitly approves.
     6→- **Versioning**: Every plan must start with a version bump per notes/README.md "Versioning during development". Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step approach before starting.
     7→- **Pre-commit checklist**: Before committing, run: tests, clippy, fmt, install the app, then retest.
     8→
     9→## Lessons Learned
    10→
    11→- **Avoid duplicate commits in .claude repo**: Committing the bot session repo multiple times in one session can create duplicate-titled commits with other session commits interleaved, making squashing non-trivial. Prefer a single commit to .claude at session end.
    12→- **ochid trailers need updating after squash**: When squashing changes the target commit in .claude, the ochid reference in the app repo must be updated to point at the correct changeID.
    13→- **Don't modify .claude repo programmatically mid-session**: Session data updates continuously, so bot-initiated changes to .claude can create circular modifications.
    14→
    15→## Key Files
    16→
    17→- notes/README.md — contains versioning workflow, todo format rules
    18→- notes/todo.md — near-term task tracking
    19→- notes/chores-01.md — chore details and reference links
    20→- CLAUDE.md — dual repo commit/push workflow
    21→
